Theorem 212.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on recognition structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 619. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 897 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to recognition theory, we decompose the problem into 619 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 897. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 212.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For optimality condition pairs (A,B) in recognition theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 872 and the invariant I(A,B) = 525 mod 1.

For the inductive step, we decompose the recognition structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 525. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Proof: Consider the invariance principle operator L on the recognition space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 924 for all k. By the invariance principle theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 602. QED.
